## Document 001: 1-10月财政数据点评：10月当月政府性基金支出同比增速加快

1-10 月财政数据点评

10 月当月政府性基金支出同比增速加快   
10 月公共预算收入同比增速加快。个人所得税对 10 月当月税收贡献明显。
公共财政仍有待向“稳增长”倾斜。10 月新增财政存款 1.4 万亿元，规模较 9
月明显抬升，有望对 11-12 月政府性基金支出形成支撑。 
 10 月公共财政收入 23379.0 亿元，同比增长 3.4%，增速较 9 月转正加快
3.5 个百分点；其中当月税收收入 21916.0 亿元，同比增长 5.9%，增速较
9 月明显加快 5.7 个百分点；非税收入 1463.0 亿元，同比下滑 23.6%，降
幅较 9 月走扩 22.5 个百分点。 
 10 月全国一般公共预算支出 17843.0 亿元，同比增长 0.5%，增速较 9 月
加快 0.4 个百分点；其中，地方公共财政支出 14055.0 亿元，同比增长 0.1%，
增速较 9 月转正加速 0.9 个百分点。 
“稳增长”方面，当月公共财政支出投向基建领域的占比为 18.2%，较 9
月回落 1.7 个百分点；具体来看，10 月公共财政投向农林水事务的支出
同比增长 6.4%，增速较上月加速 6.6 个百分点，是唯一正增长的基建相
关支出分项；当期公共财政投向城乡社区事务及交通运输领域的支出同
比增速均为负，公共财政仍有待向基建领域倾斜。 
 10 月中央政府性基金收入 875.0 亿元，同比增长 155.8 %，增速较 9 月明
显改善 142.1 个百分点；地方政府性基金收入 3238.0 亿元，同比下滑
5.2%，降幅较 9 月收窄 13.3 个百分点；国有土地使用权出让收入 2439.0
亿元，同比下降 9.0%，降幅收窄 14.2 个百分点。 
 10 月政府性基金支出 6992.0 亿元，同比增长 17.2%，增幅较 9 月明显改
善 29.5 个百分点；中央政府性基金支出 537.0 亿元，同比增长 13.3%，增
速较 9 月加快 5.8 个百分点；地方政府性基金预算支出 6455.0 亿元，同
比增长 17.5 %，增速较 9 月明显加快 30.6 个百分点；国有土地使用权出
让收入相关支出 2354.0 亿元，同比下降 0.6%，降幅较 9 月收窄 18.1 个百
分点。 
 10 月政府性基金支出同比增速加快。1-10 月广义财政支出累计同比降幅
为 2.9%，降幅较 9 月收窄 0.6 个百分点，但仍相对偏弱。中央财政支出
在 1-10 月广义财政支出中占比 13.7%，仍是重要支撑。1-10 月广义中央
财政支出同比下滑 5.7%，降幅较 9 月有所收窄，但对当期广义财政支出
同比增速仍有拖累。 
从不同预算看，首先，1-10 月一般公共预算支出在全年公共预算支出中
占比 76.0%，支出力度整体不弱，持平于去年同期；但 10 月当月支出在
全年公共预算支出中占比为 5.9%，仍低于 2024-2025 年同期水平，10 月
公共财政支出节奏仍较克制。其次，10 月政府性基金支出同比增长
17.2%，增速明显加快。 
融资方面，10 月政府债务融资节奏加快，当月社融口径政府债融资规模
1.1 万亿元，较去年同期增加 6983.0 亿元；值得一提的是，10 月新增财
政存款 1.4 万亿元，规模较 9 月明显抬升，有望对 11-12 月政府性基金支
出形成支撑。 
 风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不
及预期。

资料来源：wind，中银证券

10 月公共预算收入同比增速加快 
财政部发布数据，2026 年 1-10 月，全国一般公共预算收入 195671.0 亿元，同比增长 4.9%，增速（较
前三季度，下同）放缓 0.2 个百分点。其中，中央一般公共预算收入 88036.0 亿元，同比增长 7.5%，
增速放缓 0.8 个百分点；地方一般公共预算本级收入 107635.0 亿元，同比增长 2.9%，增速小幅加快
0.2 个百分点。 
单月数据方面，10 月公共财政收入 23379.0 亿元，同比增长 3.4%，增速较 9 月转正加快 3.5 个百分
点；其中当月税收收入 21916.0 亿元，同比增长 5.9%，增速较 9 月明显加快 5.7 个百分点；非税收
入 1463.0 亿元，同比下滑 23.6%，降幅较 9 月走扩 22.5 个百分点。

图表 2.财政收入累计同比增速表现  图表 3. 税收、非税收入当月同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

个人所得税对 10 月当月税收贡献明显。首先，国内增值税仍是占比第一大的税收品种。10 月当月，
国内增值税同比下滑 0.1%，增速较 9 月有所下滑，我们认为，增值税同比增速短期或受企业采购、
税款入库时点错位等因素影响，单月波动或属正常现象。值得注意的是，10 月 PPI、工业增加值当
月同比分别增长 5.7%和 4.8%，有望支撑当月工业企业产值，后续国内增值税同比增速有望回升。 
其次，10 月进口环节增值税和消费税同比增长 17.8%，拉动当月税收同比增速 2.4 个百分点；据海
关总署数据显示，10 月我国进口同比增速为 41.6%，对进口环节增值税收入形成支撑。 
此外，10 月国内消费税收入同比增长 4.1%，拉动当月税收同比增速 0.4 百分点；值得说明的是，消费税
收入尚不具备持续修复的基础， 10 月社零同比增速仅为 0.4%，较 9 月放缓，国内需求仍较弱势。 
其他权重税种方面，10 月个人所得税收入同比增长 89.5%，对当月税收同比增速的正贡献为 10.4 个
百分点，贡献明显，10 月个人所得税增量主要来自离岸信托个人所得税补缴。

2026 年 11 月 21 日 1-10 月财政数据点评 3

图表 4. 权重税种收入累计同比增速表现  图表 5.地产相关税种累计同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

公共财政仍有待向“稳增长”倾斜 
2026 年 1-10 月，全国一般公共预算支出 228042.0 亿元，同比增长 1.0%，增幅（较前三季度，下同）
持平；其中，中央一般公共预算本级支出 36608.0 亿元，同比增长 5.4%，增幅收窄 0.4 个百分点；
地方一般公共预算支出 191434.0 亿元，同比增长 0.2%，增幅持平。

图表 6.财政支出累计同比增速表现  图表 7. 2026 年 1-10 月财政支出同比增速结构变化

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

单月数据方面，10 月全国一般公共预算支出 17843.0 亿元，同比增长 0.5%，增速较 9 月加快 0.4
个百分点；其中，地方公共财政支出 14055.0 亿元，同比增长 0.1%，增速较 9 月转正加速 0.9 个
百分点。 
“稳增长”方面，当月公共财政支出投向基建领域的占比为 18.2%，较 9 月回落 1.7 个百分点；具体
来看，10 月公共财政投向农林水事务的支出同比增长 6.4%，增速较上月加速 6.6 个百分点，是唯一
正增长的基建相关支出分项；当期公共财政投向城乡社区事务及交通运输领域的支出同比增速均为
负，公共财政仍有待向基建领域倾斜。 
此外，教育、社保就业支出维持较强刚性，均仍为 10 月公共财政支出的重点方向，占比分别为 14.8%
和 15.8%。

### Page 4

2026 年 11 月 21 日 1-10 月财政数据点评 4

图表 8. 社保就业相关支出累计同比增速表现  图表 9.基建领域相关支出累计同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

土地收入对 1-10 月地方政府性基金收入仍有拖累 
2026 年 1-10 月，全国政府性基金预算收入 29123.0 亿元，同比下降 15.5%，降幅（较前三季度，下
同）收窄 3.1 个百分点。分中央和地方看，中央政府性基金预算收入 4557.0 亿元，同比增长 25.9%，
增幅加速 13.5 个百分点；地方政府性基金预算本级收入 24566.0 亿元，同比下滑 20.4%，降幅收窄
1.9 个百分点，其中，国有土地使用权出让收入 18526.0 亿元，同比下降 25.8%，降幅收窄 2.1 个百
分点，但需注意的是，土地收入对 1-10 月地方政府性基金收入仍有较明显拖累，负贡献为 20.9 个百
分点。 
单月数据方面，10 月中央政府性基金收入 875.0 亿元，同比增长 155.8 %，增速较 9 月明显改善 142.1
个百分点；地方政府性基金收入 3238.0 亿元，同比下滑 5.2%，降幅较 9 月收窄 13.3 个百分点；国
有土地使用权出让收入 2439.0 亿元，同比下降 9.0%，降幅收窄 14.2 个百分点。

图表 10. 政府性基金预算收入当月规模表现  图表 11. 地方政府性基金预算收入当月同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

支出方面，2026 年 1-10 月，全国政府性基金预算支出 69746.0 亿元，同比下滑 13.8%，降幅（相较
于前三季度，下同）收窄 2.4 个百分点；中央本级政府性基金支出 4229.0 亿元，同比下降 50.6%，
降幅较上月收窄 3.8 个百分点，中央财政支出力度相对温和；地方政府性基金预算支出 65517.0 亿元，
同比下降 9.4%，降幅收窄 2.2 个百分点，国有土地使用权出让收入相关支出 27919.0 亿元，同比下
降 17.3%，降幅收窄 1.2 个百分点。

### Page 5

2026 年 11 月 21 日 1-10 月财政数据点评 5

单月数据方面，10 月政府性基金支出 6992.0 亿元，同比增长 17.2%，增幅较 9 月明显改善 29.5 个百
分点；中央政府性基金支出 537.0 亿元，同比增长 13.3%，增速较 9 月加快 5.8 个百分点；地方政府
性基金预算支出 6455.0 亿元，同比增长 17.5 %，增速较 9 月明显加快 30.6 个百分点；国有土地使用
权出让收入相关支出 2354.0 亿元，同比下降 0.6%，降幅较 9 月收窄 18.1 个百分点。

图表 12. 政府性基金预算支出当月规模表现  图表 13. 政府性基金预算支出当月同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

10 月政府性基金支出同比增速加快。1-10 月广义财政支出累计同比降幅为 2.9%，降幅较 9 月收窄
0.6 个百分点，但仍相对偏弱。中央财政支出在 1-10 月广义财政支出中占比 13.7%，仍是重要支撑。
1-10 月广义中央财政支出同比下滑 5.7%，降幅较 9 月有所收窄，但对当期广义财政支出同比增速仍
有拖累。 
从不同预算看，首先，1-10 月一般公共预算支出在全年公共预算支出中占比 76.0%，支出力度整体
不弱，持平于去年同期；但 10 月当月支出在全年公共预算支出中占比为 5.9%，仍低于 2024-2025
年同期水平，10 月公共财政支出节奏仍较克制。其次，10 月政府性基金支出同比增长 17.2%，增速
明显加快。 
融资方面，10 月政府债务融资节奏加快，当月社融口径政府债融资规模 1.1 万亿元，较去年同期增
加 6983.0 亿元；值得一提的是，10 月新增财政存款 1.4 万亿元，规模较 9 月明显抬升，有望对 11-12
月政府性基金支出形成支撑。 
风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不及预期。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。

790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。

4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額 534 億 元 ， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

## Document 040: 20261109_投資週報_期中選舉藍色浪潮，AI基建疑慮升溫

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261109_投資週報_期中選舉藍色浪潮，AI基建疑慮升溫.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 11 月 09 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 期中選舉「藍色浪潮」，AI 基建政策風險升溫：11 月 3 日美國期中選舉民主黨奪回參眾兩院，
德州新任州長揚言暫停資料中心建設，市場擔憂電力、用水與環境審查趨嚴，AI 資本支出前景
遭重新評價；加上 Fed 官員釋出續升息訊號，資金撤出高評價半導體與 AI 基建族群，美股科
技股大幅修正。短線政策雜音難消，但 AI 需求本質未變，後續觀察新國會實際立法方向。
◼ 科技財報喜憂參半，台股賣壓沉重、政府出手穩市：蘋果營收優於預期但毛利率受記憶體成本

壓抑，AMD 資料中心營收近倍增、高通財報後走弱。美股賣壓延燒，台股連日重挫、新台幣
貶破 33 元，行政院指示金管會機動因應並研議穩定股市措施，官股行庫與投信持續買超。看
法：短線波動仍大，但基本面與政策支撐有助指數逐步築底。
◼ 美國製造業續擴張，台灣通膨降溫：美國 10 月 ISM 製造業指數升至 55.5，連續第十個月擴
張，惟價格分項仍處高檔；台灣 10 月製造業 PMI 連續 13 個月擴張但速度趨緩，CPI 年增率
降至 2.33%。看法：美國景氣與通膨黏性支撐 Fed 偏鷹立場，台灣央行壓力相對減輕；後續
關注 10 月非農就業、11 月 10 日美國 CPI 及 12 月 FOMC 會議。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：科技股中期成長趨勢仍具支撐，
惟短線宜重視個股分化，美國期中選舉帶來的政策不確定性，短期可能使科技股評價承壓，
惟大型雲端業者持續投入 AI 基礎建設，半導體及相關設備需求仍具中期成長動能，科技股整
體基本面仍具支撐，但短期應避免過度集中於高評價、高資本支出及高度依賴外部融資的企

業。優先關注具備技術競爭優勢、穩定營運現金流及較高投資回報能見度的企業。
◼ 中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債仍較具優勢：在美國政策風險升溫及科技股評價調整期
間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延
長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置
穩定性上仍較具優勢。信用債方面，營運現金流充足、槓桿適中及再融資需求可控的發行人
仍較為穩健，對 AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

## Document 100: 2027年度宏观经济展望：宏观向好，结构向新向优向绿

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年度宏观经济展望：宏观向好，结构向新向优向绿.pdf
Report source: 上海证券
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 47
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告
2026年11月20日
行业：宏观
宏观向好，结构向新向优向绿
——2027年度宏观经济展望
分析师：陈彦利      SAC编号：S0870517070002
分析师：韦磊          SAC编号：S0870525030003

### Page 2

2
主要观点
u 海外经济表现分化，美国经济韧性，其中投资贡献上升，净出口拖累扩大。AI投资拉动了美国的私人投资，同时由于依赖
设备的进口，也加大了净出口的跌幅。美国2026年以来通胀水平明显高于2025年，受地缘政治因素带来油价上涨，仍是当
前美国通胀的主要症结。就业市场稳定向好，通胀压力下，美国开启加息。
u 国内经济外强内弱。进出口双强，投资消费偏弱。油价对于CPI的影响上升，工业品价格上下游均有所涨价。流动性充裕，
金融总量合理增长。社会综合融资成本低位运行，信贷结构优化。财政收入修复，支出偏慢，债务付息仍是负担。前三季
度4.6%的经济增速，有望顺利完成全年经济增速目标。
u 我们认为2027年的经济增速仍可能保持在4.5%-5%的区间。2027年作为十五五第二年，我们认为也有望延续26年的基调，改
革与发展并重，既要有空间，也要为后期夯实基础。2027年出口仍有望延续景气，出口动能继续向新向优，AI投资浪潮下
，高技术产品出口仍是重要支撑。投资方面预计仍受到房地产投资的拖累，制造业投资方面呈现结构分化，高技术产业投

资发展较快。消费方面，服务消费或更值得关注。适应居民消费结构升级，实施服务业扩能提质行动。
u 宏观向好，结构向新向优向绿。发展新质生产力是推动高质量发展的内在要求和重要着力点。
风险提示：地缘政治事件恶化；贸易摩擦加剧；AI投资进展超预期放缓；主要经济体货币政策超预期变化

3.4 美国加息压力犹存，但步调审慎
u 从美联储公布的最新点阵图来看，年内还有加息一次的可能，27年再加息一次后，随后将逐渐降息
u 我们认为地缘政治因素引发的通胀的高位致使美国存在加息的压力，但整体来看步调温和审慎，并非政策大幅反转，影响
将可控

### Page 35

35
3.5 宏观总量向好，结构调整不断优化
u 前三季度4.6%的经济增速，且三季度经济增速有所反弹，有望顺利完成全年经济增速目标
u 十五五开局之年采用了区间目标是“为调结构、防风险、促改革留出空间，为后期更好发展打牢基础”，而2027年作为第二
年，我们认为也有望延续26年的基调，改革与发展并重，既要有空间，也要为后期夯实基础：
u 2027年GDP仍有望在4.5%-5%，宏观总量向好。
u 2027年出口仍有望延续景气，AI投资浪潮下，高技术产品出口仍是重要支撑。
u 投资方面预计仍受到房地产投资的拖累，制造业投资方面呈现结构分化，高技术产业投资发展较快。
u 消费方面，服务消费或更值得关注。适应居民消费结构升级，实施服务业扩能提质行动。

应量增长同经济增长和价格总水平预期目标相匹配

新、中小微企业、“六张网”建设等重点领域的金融支持

## Document 155: 261117_ubs_BIS-control

Source PDF: all report pdf/261117_ubs_BIS-control.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 17 November 2026
Global Research
UBS Global I/O Semiconductors
New US Export Controls on Cloud Access;
Assessing the Impact
Summary Thoughts
The US Dept. of Commerce Bureau of Industry and Security (BIS) posted a new rule
addressing remote access to advanced semiconductors. Following discussions with
industry experts (including a former Commerce Dept. official), we believe the rule  is
essentially attempting to accomplish the same thing as the AI Diffusion Rule issued by

the Biden Administration, but never put into effect. We would note that the rule was
"written by, and at the direction of the  Department of Treasury" - atypical for such a
rule, according to our experts. While we can't say for sure that this is in direct response
to the NVDA situation, the timing seems highly coincidental and we highlighted this risk
here and in our estimate cuts yesterday morning. We would make a few key points:
 Demand impact is hard to assess, but could be significant. Current export

control laws have not required a license  for shipments to key countries such as
Malaysia, Thailand, Indonesia, and Singapore (though we note  there is little room
for data centers in Singapore). As a result, we have seen a large increase in data
center construction in Malaysia in particular with recent press reports from the
NYT and Economist  (here and here) suggesting ~2GW of new data centers now
online in a single province in Malaysia with another ~10GW currently under

construction, and  total approved volume up to ~25GW with backing from both US
and China hyperscalers. On this basis, it seems fair to assume  a  run-rate for
shipments into Malaysia alone of a few GW/yr and the NYT article indicated that
about half of this is going to China-domiciled  companies. There is a carve-out in
the new rule for online retailers and social media companies but we would think
that US companies would err on the side of conservatism unless they can be

continuously assured of the end customer. Ergo, it is possible that this new rule
could strip 1-2GW out of current demand, with most of this likely being from
NVDA. At $30B+/GW, this is roughly consistent with our estimate of a 10%
demand impact, though NVDA should still be able to divert these shipments to
other customers to mitigate any near-term impact so we see this as more of a
longer-term issue especially if powered shell permitting gets more restrictive in the

US.
 Odds of MATCH Act passage have gone up, but timing remains a key Q. We
have written in the past about  MATCH  here and here. While the US may not want
to enforce additional export controls on the heels of this new cloud access rule,
our conversations with experts marked a clear shift in tone around the odds of the
MATCH Act passing vs. prior months - with the legislation potentially being
attached to the NDAA in this upcoming Congress. That being said, an expert firmly

believes that the disparity between what ASML, TEL, and other foreign equipment
suppliers currently sell into China vs. US SPE suppliers is not all that significant,
making the rule more of a point of tension with US allies such as the Netherlands
and Japan. Second, the goal of this legislation would be "level the playing field"
using the FDPR (Foreign Direct Product Rule) but there is a question of who would
determine which tools sold by the Dutch and Japanese suppliers are “equivalent”

to those currently restricted for US equipment suppliers. Third, a contemplated
ban on immersion DUV would make supporting trailing-edge fabs and lines in
China quite challenging, with potential spillover effects across the industry, even
outside China. Fourth, it raises the question of what incentives, if any, the Dutch
and Japanese governments would have to comply. Related to this, an expert
indicated that the use of FDPR by the USG could be more upsetting than tariffs

### Page 2

directly impacted by this rule, but in our coverage, we see KLAC being the only one
really indirectly negatively affected by the passage of this rule given its high attach
to litho process steps (and others affected to a lesser extent, though less litho
could in fact ripple to fewer dep/etch tools).
Read Across
We see NVDA as the most directly impacted by this rule, though the entire supply chain,
from memory through SPE, would ultimately be negatively affected by the closure of

such a large demand corridor. That said, we could argue that, over the longer term,
approving  the MATCH Act could benefit memory suppliers outside China by
constraining domestic Chinese capacity additions.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

MediaTek  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Potential to win next gen TPU projects, while other ASIC
customers remain undisclosed.
1. Major AI ASIC project (TPU) - potential to win next generation projects.
• TPU v8 shipment track, US$2.5bn revenue in 4Q26. Reiterated US$16bn
revenue in 2027, high end of guidance. As there are more customers than
chip supply, if ABF substrate supply improves, revenue can improve.
• TPU v9 EMIB-T substrate supply is also key, company is comfortable on yield

and output trajectory.
• It sees its TPU v9 project as a major TPU SKUs for the customer. MediaTek
revenue per chip is higher than it was in v8. The compute die is handled by
the customer.
• MediaTek said it is possible for the customer to use designed TPU (eg. v8t
and v9) for AI LLM customer's rack system, besides Google's internal use.
• On third generation (TPU v10), MediaTek is working with customer's internal
team, and has high confidence to win the project.

• New generation TPU compute die handled by customer. MediaTek will focus
on D2D inference, I/O die, and chip packaging technology.
• We maintain our view that Alchip is strong candidate for TPU compute die
backend design. Based on Alchip’s NRE revenue timing, we think the
compute die could be for the TPU v10.
2. Other ASIC customers - awaiting more disclosure: MediaTek still cannot
disclose the non-China CSP customer name or project details. The engagement with

China CSP is ongoing, but needs to comply with US BIS rules.
3. ASIC business financial profile - OP margin improvement: Mediatek said a major
customer wanted to drive to full COT (customer own tool), but with the chip's
complexity growing, it hopes to sustain ASIC margin in future generations. The
company reiterated its 3Q earning call comments that ASIC op. margin is 10% above
the previous average. It reiterated its op margin can improve to 20%+ in 2027 on

higher ASIC revenue contribution. We estimate ASIC op. margin at 25-30%.
4. Smartphone business - still challenging: High memory costs continue to impact
China demand. Blended ASP for MediaTek's SoC has risen on price hikes and better
product mix.
Reiterate OW & Top Pick: MediaTek continues to win new TPU projects with higher
revenue per chip, and so revenue growth may extend to 2030-31. The chance to
supply to AI LLM for end-customer's rack system via a major customer could also

the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
MediaTek (2454.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity 9.2%, intermediate growth
rate 12.0%, terminal growth rate 3.0%.
Risks to Upside
n Edge AI proliferates smartphone replacement cycle.
n Smartphone demand rises in China and other EMs.
n New products attract high demand, resulting in market share gains.
n Google's TPU spurs demand.
Risks to Downside

n Smartphone demand deteriorates in China and other EMs.
n Competition heats up, resulting in pricing competition.
n New products attract low demand, causing market share loss.
n Margin dilution is more severe.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

研 究 報 告                
旺矽(6223.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
探針卡需求強勁挹注短期營運動能強勁、CPO 設備進度如期  
 投資評價與建議 
目前探針卡廠商評價普遍修正至20~35倍，尤其以短期營運相對穩健之廠商
(如精測、旺矽)仍相對有撐，而我們觀察公司探針卡需求強勁支撐短期營運動
能強勁，並重申CPO設備驗證進度如期、客戶數持續增加，故仍看好評價仍有
望回到合理區間之中上緣。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率
維持近3Q26之高檔水準，觀察探針卡BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，且擴產
計畫不變。基於公司10月營收達22.3億元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，
預估營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年：
(1)VPC和MEMS明年底有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，擴充幅度仍具有上
修空間，當前VPC主要客戶未有升級至MEMS規劃，至於MEMS主要貢獻
則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU；(2)insertion 2客戶將於12月通知驗證結
果，1H27有望獲得量產機訂單，至於insertion 3已如期出貨工程機給3間
客戶，並預期1H27將陸續獲得量產機訂單，惟公司預估CPO設備營收顯
著貢獻時間點將落在2028年，故我們同步調整營收、獲利。 
 投資風險 
CPO進展不如預期、探針卡擴產針數下修。

### Page 2

| 營運概況 
3Q26獲利優於預期：3Q26營收季增12.8%至59億元，連續七季創下歷史新高，毛利率61.7%，稅後EPS 19.18元，
其中營收、獲利均優於預期，推估主因為探針卡產品組合改善，加上於2Q26進行之探針卡報價調整完整貢獻3Q
營收(包含CPC漲價20%、取消VPC和MEMS 10%價格優惠)，另公司營業費用管控得宜也同步挹注獲利。 
估4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率有望維持近3Q26之高檔水準，我們
推估動能包含：(1)維持VPC、MEMS月產能今年底分別擴至200萬針、350萬針之進展；(2)訂單出貨則觀察探針卡
BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，其中CPC今年前10個月維持滿載、目前BB Ratio接近1，至於MEMS、VPC則超過1，
考量公司探針卡交期已明顯延⻑至4~6個月，隱含本季探針卡營收仍有望持續提升。基於公司10月營收達22.3億
元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，預估本季營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年，我們觀察：(1)探針卡中，明年CPC月產能維
持50萬針、VPC和MEMS明年底則有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，產能擴充幅度具有上修空間，且強調2028年維持
積極擴產策略，訂單貢獻中，VPC主要客戶近年未有升級至MEMS探針卡之規劃，預期該客戶AI ASIC訂單將支撐
明年VPC需求，至於MEMS明年主要貢獻則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU，且公司持續爭取潛在客戶AI ASIC訂單，
另規劃於2H27量產之PCB廠有望挹注探針卡成本優化；(2)CPO設備中，insertion 2如我們先前所提，於3Q26底
～4Q26初送樣至客戶端進行驗證，公司預估客戶將於12月通知驗證結果，根據目前客戶回饋正向，預期1H27有
望獲得量產機訂單，此外近期有另⼀客戶與公司接洽中(公司預估1H27獲得驗證結果)，至於insertion 3則已如期
出貨工程機給3間客戶，也將陸續交機給另3間客戶，預計1H27將陸續獲得insertion 3量產機訂單，惟考量接單至
出貨、裝機驗收之時間較⻑，公司認為CPO相關設備顯著營收貢獻時間點將落在2028年，我們則同步下修明年相
關設備之營收認列數量。綜上所述，公司預估明年營收有望年增至少50%、毛利率維持在60%以上；我們則預估
明年營收有望年增58.6%至344.6億元，較先前下修主要反映部分CPO設備遞延至2028年認列營收，毛利率63.2%，
稅後EPS 117.77元。

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

新代(7750)：中國高階控制器需求持續強勁 
建議：評等買進

一、新代為台灣精密設備控制器
大廠 
 新代成立於 1995 年，為專注於精密設備控制器之大廠，以自有品牌 SYNTEC
行銷全球。主營產品涵蓋 CNC 數控系統、伺服產品與產業專機；集團亦積極佈
局新成長動能，旗下「聯達智能」與「正鉑雷射」分別專攻機器人/雲端智造
（IoT/MES）及工業減碳製程設備。公司具備兩大核心優勢：其一為精準的市場
定位，產品品質媲美德日大廠（如 Siemens、Fanuc），同時具備極佳的性價比，
成功填補高階進口與中低階國產間的市場缺口；其二為軟硬整合與在地服務，透
過橫跨歐美、亞太及中國 24 個分公司的全球據點，提供即時服務以協助機械廠
進行差異化升級。目前新代服務逾 2,000 家客戶，單一客戶營收占比均低於 5%，
客戶結構極具分散性與營運韌性，築起穩固的長期競爭護城河。

二、產品組合優化抵銷原物料成
本上漲，3Q26 毛利維持約 44%
水準 
 受惠中國工具機及金屬加工產業需求強勁、終端客戶持續升級高階控制器，以及
競爭對手關鍵零組件供應受限帶動轉單，新代 3Q26 營收達 78.05 億元，QoQ-
5.5%、YoY+157.7%，雖受傳統淡季影響較上季下滑，但仍創歷史單季次高。獲
利方面， 儘管記憶體價格上漲，受惠高階控制器銷售比重提升及產品組合優化，
3Q26 毛利率達 44.44%，季增 0.35 個百分點、年減 0.04 個百分點，維持公司
長期指引 45%±2%區間； 隨營收規模擴大及費用率改善，3Q26 營業利益達
21.06 億元，QoQ-11.9%、YoY+398.2%，營業利益率達 26.98%，年增 13.02
個百分點；稅後淨利達 16.80 億元，QoQ-11.4%、YoY+360.2%，單季 EPS 達
24.06 元，創歷史單季次高。1-3Q26 稅後淨利達 46.50 億元，YoY+221.7%，
EPS 達 66.59 元，反映高階產品需求成長、轉單效益及營運規模擴張持續挹注
獲利。

三、中國高階控制器滲透率持續
提升，海外布局與產能擴張挹注
2027 年成長動能 
 2026 年中國工具機產業維持成長動能，1–8 月金屬切削相關產業成長約 18–
19%，受惠製造業升級、五軸加工及車銑複合機等高階設備需求增加，帶動控制
器與伺服系統銷售。新代憑藉高階產品性能、較國際競品約 20%的價格優勢，
以及中國逾 2,000 家活躍客戶與在地直銷服務網絡，持續擴大市占率，2026 年
控制器出貨量預估 YoY+70–80%，並受惠日系競爭對手關鍵零組件供應受限，
取得大型工具機客戶轉單。儘管部分日系廠商供貨逐步恢復，公司透過提前備貨
及與記憶體原廠簽訂供應協議，預期關鍵零組件供應可維持至 2027 年下半年，
搭配產品組合優化及客戶黏著度提升，有望延續競爭優勢。海外布局方面，印度
市場受惠製造業在地化政策及工具機產業升級需求，帶動高階控制器國產替代
與供應商多元化商機。公司已與印度前十大工具機廠建立合作接洽，並取得兩筆
單筆超過 100 套的訂單，預期 2026 年印度營收 YoY+100%，惟目前營收貢獻

導入，帶動營收與獲利持續成長。

四、評等買進理由  綜合前述，展望 2027 年，新代受惠中國高階工具機需求成長、日系競爭對手轉
單效益延續，以及控制器市占率持續提升，營運成長動能可望延續。隨蘇州二廠
及馬來西亞新廠陸續投產，預計自 2Q27 起新增產能逐步開出，整體控制器產能
有望擴充至目前約 2.5 倍，搭配印度大型工具機客戶導入加速，以及多合一高階
控制器與智慧自動化產品持續推出，有助推升產品 ASP 及獲利能力，預估 2026
年稅 EPS 為 91.49，預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 為 118.49。至於評價面部分，過往
3 年 PER 平均落在 25x-35x，目前約 16x，處歷史區間下緣，中國市占率提升、
印度市場拓展、新產能貢獻及智慧製造業務成長等中長期動能，認為目前評價具
吸引力，故給予買進評等，目標價 2300 元(25x2026EPS)。

7788.TT 松川精密 
 趨勢不變 評價調升

[財務資訊更新，調升評價]   
   公司簡介 松川精密成立於 1974 年，主要從事繼電器之研發、製造及銷售，繼電器屬於自動控 制開關的一種，係以較小電流(訊號)連動大電流(電源)的元件。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
強力買進 348 
【前次投資建議】買進，348 
【大盤指數/股價】41040 / 178.5

➢ 高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元  
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利
率 27.97%，稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 
2.83 元，低於投研部預期，主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含
兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，
且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個百分點；2)高價原
料成本影響：上半年白銀曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史天價，公司 8~9
月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料。 
➢ 最壞情況已過，4Q26 獲利重返單季新高 
10 月營收 8.49 億元，MoM-3.19%，YoY+53.62%，達成率 32%，符
合預期，部分產品出貨受船期影響，致營收遞延認列，整體單季營收與
原先預估差異不大。毛利率方面，下半年白銀每季平均跌幅 12%~14%，
目前回落至 50 美元以下，原料平均成本已下降，10 月高價原料衝擊影
響性降低，毛利率回升，預期單季最壞情況已過，4Q26 毛利率回升至
29.11%，EPS 3.33 元，獲利重返成長。 
➢ AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，必須額外配置
高壓直流接觸器，技術門檻遠高於傳統產品，單價達到交流繼電器的 20
倍至 30 倍，投研部推測每機櫃至少需配置 12 至 28 顆不等，單機櫃產
值成長 198%。松川 225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，直流接
觸器將是松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼
電器/接觸器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
➢ 評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
投研部預估松川精密 2026 年/2027 年 EPS 分別為 11.68 元及 15.58
元，2027 年各應用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利
率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優
於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方面，目前松川本益比僅 11
倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投研部將松川投
資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元，低於預期 
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利率 27.97%，
稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 2.83 元，低於投研部預期，
主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台
興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個
百分點；2)高價原料成本衝擊：白銀在 2026 上半年曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史
天價，公司 8~9 月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料，對 8~9 月的單月毛利率造成短期壓抑。

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
民主黨勝選有利綠能產業發展，儲能剛性需求強 
根據產業統計，2026 年前三季全球儲能總併網量估計達 220 至 240 GWh，系統出貨
量約 420 GWh。受惠於第四季併網旺季，BloombergNEF 於 10 月將 2026 全年新增
裝機預估上調至 188 GW / 535 GWh，並預期 2027 年將進一步成長至 209 GW / 638 
GWh，容量年增 19.3%。太陽能方面，全球新增裝機在 2025 年創高後，2026 年受
中國電網消納限制影響，預計微幅回落至 584 至 638 GW；但海外市場逆勢成長 15%
至 313 GW。展望 2027 年，全球裝機預估將回升至 618 GW，產業呈現「光弱儲強」
趨勢，加速光儲一體化建置。 
綜合上述，全球光儲兩大核心市場皆集中於中美兩國，加上美國民主黨於 2026 年期
中選舉勝出並奪下國會主導權，綠能與清潔能源政策延續性獲得有力支撐，可望進一
步推升當地光儲建置動能。中美兩地領導廠商均為松川精密的核心客戶，受惠於美系
客戶新一代儲能櫃容量由 3.9 MWh 提升至 5 MWh，工作電流增加帶動接觸器規格與
單價雙雙看漲，預估 2027 年松川在儲能、太陽能及微電網業務將維持 10%~15%的
成長。 
AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，機櫃配電單元(PDU)面臨嚴
峻的高壓電弧安全挑戰，必須額外配置高壓直流接觸器(DC Contactor)，為在毫秒內
強制切斷(滅弧)800V 高壓直流電弧，產品內部高度仰賴磁吹滅弧設計或真空密封充氣
(氫氣／氮氣)工藝，技術門檻遠高於傳統交流繼電器(AC Relay)，單價達到交流繼電器
的 20 倍至 30 倍。在用量方面，每座機櫃 PDU 至少需配置 12 顆，主流高階規格更
需 24 顆至 28 顆，單機櫃直流接觸器產值為既有交流繼電器的 2 至 3 倍。松川
225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，未來隨著高壓直流架構全面放量，直流接
觸器將正式擔綱松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎。在額外增加直流接觸器需求下，投研
部推測高壓直流架構(不含自動轉換開關 ATS)，AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸器單機櫃產值將
從 7,000 元增加至 20,900 元，YoY+198%，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸
器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
預估松川 2027 年 EPS 15.58 元，評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
預估松川精密 2026 年營收 95.52 億元，YoY+48.49%，毛利率 28.80%，稅後淨利
9.31 億元，YoY+246.03%，EPS 11.68 元；預估 2027 年 113.14 億元，YoY+18.45%，
毛利率 30.53%，稅後淨利 12.43 億元，YoY+33.42%，EPS 15.58 元。2027 年各應
用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修
直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方
面，目前松川本益比僅 11 倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投
研部將松川投資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

## Document 257: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% YoY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% YoY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% YoY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

자료: 유안타증권

Company Report  2026.11.12

상선, 방산, 해양, 엔진 모든 사업 영위하는 전천후 기업 
경쟁사 대비 1) 가장 넓은 사업 영역을 보유, 2) 시가총액 또한 높음. 이러한 특성은 동사의 
사업 포트폴리오 균형 관점에서 장점이나, 반대로 투자 매력의 집중을 일정 부분 희석. 경쟁
사 대비 주가 상승 트리거가 가장 폭넓게 존재하나, 상승 탄력은 그만큼 제한. 
상선: FY27F 매출인식은 FY23-25 수주 물량, 가스선 매출비중 하락 
FY27F 상선부문 매출은 FY23-25 수주 물량이 인식되며 (일부 FY26F 수주), FY25 수주
한 물량이 연간 40% 수준의 가장 높은 매출기여도를 보일 것. 2H27F부터 올해 수주한 일
부 선박의 매출인식 또한 발생하며, 연중 가스선 매출 비중이 하락하고 컨테이너선 매출 비
중이 상승. 
방산: 좋게 보자면 불안하고, 나쁘게 보자면 아쉬운 
MASGA를 국내 야드에서 진행한다는 대전제로 경쟁사와 같은 적정가치를 부여해 왔으나, 
미국 현지 건조와 같은 여러 가능성을 고려하면 다음과 같은 불확실성 존재: 1) 중간지주사
와의 미국내 JV 설립 관련 투자 지분 미정, 2) 신규 조선소 건립시 미국내 수주와 매출까지 
걸리는 시간. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 다 깎아서 봐도 충분한 상승여력 
상선부문 26.5조, 방산 8조, 선박엔진 9조, 발전엔진 (FY30F 기준) 13.5조 수준의 SOTP 
밸류에이션 기반하여 목표주가 643,000원 제시. 발전엔진 증설로 인한 미래가치를 반영하
나, 상선부문 Target P/O multiple 하향 (0.7x, 업종 공통)과 MASGA 적정가치 하향으로 
목표주가 자체는 하락. 보수적 멀티플 제시함에도 주가 상승 여력은 충분히 확보된 상황. 업
종 전체적 긍정요인 외의 동사 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 미국 현지 조선소 투자주체 발탁, 2) 
DC향 발전엔진 추가 수주, 3) MASGA 국내 건조 물량 수주.

## Document 277: AI巨头CEO惊人暴论：人类被AI抢饭碗后，可以用沉浸式游戏安抚

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI巨头CEO惊人暴论：人类被AI抢饭碗后，可以用沉浸式游戏安抚.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic CEO 达⾥奥早年曾提出⽤ “ 沉浸式电⼦游戏 ” 安抚 AI 导致的⼤规模失业者。与此同
时，公司被曝内部⽂化近乎 “ 邪教 ” ，将⾃⼰视为拯救⼈类的救世主。⼀边⾼举 AI 安全旗帜，
⼀边争夺巨额财富与⾏业话语权， Anthropic 的道德标兵⼈设正遭遇质疑。
摘要：
IPO 不久， Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei 就被翻出了「暴论」。
AI 让⼈类失业怎么办？
那就让他们来玩沉浸式电⼦游戏，防⽌⼤规模动荡！
今天，不少外媒报道了 Dario 曾经的这⼀⾔论。
对外， Anthropic 是⼀家强调「道德、安全、⼈类福祉」的公司。
但其实他们对普通⼈的态度，竟然如此傲慢？
⼀句「游戏维稳论」，让全⽹怒了
11 ⽉ 22 ⽇，外媒报道： Dario 早年在 OpenAI 任职期间，曾在内部 Slack 群聊中，与同事展开过⼀
场激烈的辩论。
当时，⼤佬们正在探讨⼀个⾮常现实的问题：如果 AGI 真的实现，把⼈类的⼯作全⼲了，导致成
百上千万普通⼈失业，进⽽引发巨⼤的社会动荡和冲突，该怎么收场？

当时在群⾥， Dario 给出的天才⽅案是：⽤「沉浸式电⼦游戏」来安抚这些失业的⼈，不就解决
了？
此⾔⼀出，外⽹直接炸锅了。
外媒 The Verge ，将这种说法痛批为「反乌托邦」。
美国知名科技媒体《 The Verge 》毫不留情地将其痛批为「反乌托邦式⽅案」。

### Page 2

⽆数⽹友在评论区开启群嘲：这简直是奶头乐的终极版。
虽然，这起事件发⽣在 Dario 还在 OpenAI ⼯作时，被解释为「仅仅是内部探讨的创意想法，⽽⾮
官⽅政策」。
但细思极恐的是，这些硅⾕底层精英，⼼⾥真是这么想的。
Dario ⾃⼰就曾公开预测， AI 可能会将美国的失业率推⾼⾄ 10%-20% 。
当被机器接管⼯作，⼈失去的不仅是薪⽔，更是⽇常的规律⽣活、社交的连接，以及⽣命的意
义。
⽽ Dario 在潜意识⾥，不需要为⼈类创造新的价值体系，⽽是⽤精神鸦⽚，把所有⼈圈养起来。
这简直就是现实版的《⿊客帝国》，他们是造物主，⽽普通⼈，只不过是躺在营养舱⾥做梦的电
池。
⾯试被问：「准备好加⼊邪教了吗？」
如果你觉得 Dario 只是偶尔「语出惊⼈」，那你就⼤错特错了。
据爆料，他⼀⼿创办的 Anthropic ，内部⽂化简直⾮常「诡异」。

### Page 3

在硅⾕， Anthropic ⼀直⽴着完美的⼈设：他们是 AI 界的道德标兵，是⼀群为了防⽌ AI 毁灭⼈类
⽽毅然离开 OpenAI 的「叛道者」。
但 Dario 这个⼈，⾏事实在太怪了。
他曾经头顶着⼀整条⾯包去参加开发者会议。
不过，相⽐接下来的事，这件事已经是最正常的⼀件了。

### Page 4

有⼈透露， Dario 患有被害妄想症。
最近 Antropic  IPO 后，  他在意⼤利被拍到奇怪的拔⽑癖 ——

### Page 5

据前同事爆料，在 OpenAI 当研究副总裁时，达⾥奥⾏事就颇为偏执。他相信 AI 如果发展过快，
有 20%-30% 的概率会毁灭⼈类，这⽐核武器还要危险。
为了防⽌所谓的「信息危害」，他甚⾄会专⻔购买物理断⽹的电脑和打印机来写⽂章，⽣怕稍微
⼀点信息泄露，「核弹配⽅」就要落⼊恐怖分⼦之⼿，世界就要毁灭了。
这种「救世主情结」，在他的⻣⼦⾥根深蒂固。
应聘者会被要求提前阅读 Dario 写的⻓篇⼤论，⼊职好⽐「加⼊邪教」 ——
在短信⾥，⼀位招聘经理甚⾄半开玩笑地问落选的候选⼈：「你准备好加⼊邪教了吗？」

### Page 6

在公司内部，员⼯们⾃称为「蚂蚁」。每两周⼀次的全员⼤会，被戏称为「达⾥奥的幻象探索」
（ Dario Vision Quests ）。
在这个会上，达⾥奥就像⼤祭司⼀样，向员⼯们输出他对 AI 、哲学甚⾄世界末⽇的⻅解。
Dario 的 14000 字 AI 宣⾔，被印成实体书发给员⼯，被戏称「⼩蓝书」。
整个公司建⽴在⼀种狂热信仰之上：我们 Anthropic ，尤其是 Dario 本⼈，最知道什么对世界是好
的；我们必须掌握巨⼤的权⼒，因为只有我们在负责任地拯救⼈类。
「这就像是⼀个曼哈顿计划⻛格的疯狂地⽅ …… ⼤家觉得⾃⼰披上了超级英雄的⽃篷，正在解
决世界上最紧迫的问题。」⼀位离职的前员⼯如此评价。
怎么还有两幅⾯孔
最让外界感到讽刺的，是 Anthropic ⼜当⼜⽴的⾏径。
他们⼀边在媒体⾯前装作痛⼼疾⾸，承诺「必要时为了安全会放缓 AI 的开发速度」，宣称绝不说
谎；另⼀边，却在资本市场狂飙，把估值炒到了惊⼈的 1.5 万亿美元！
⽽且， Dario 这位「道德标兵」，翻脸⽐翻书还快。

⽐如，他曾对内部⼤放厥词，痛骂英伟达 CEO ⻩仁勋「贪婪」。
原因就在于，⽼⻩⽀持开源。所以即使依赖着英伟达的 GPU 来训练⾃⼰的⼤模型，⽼⻩也依旧
该骂。
难怪⽼⻩以前会开炮，讽刺某些⼈有救世主情结。

## Document 279: AI算力市场的危险循环：越借不到钱，GPU租金反而越贵

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI算力市场的危险循环：越借不到钱，GPU租金反而越贵.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

贷款机构收紧 neocloud 融资，将贷款价值⽐从 80% 降⾄ 50% ⾄ 60% ，迫使买家转向现货市
场，反⽽推⾼ GPU 租⾦。租⾦上涨⼜⽀撑 GPU 资产估值，但初创公司的算⼒采购仍依赖⻛
投资⾦， token 价格却持续下跌，融资收缩与需求脆弱可能形成恶性循环。
摘要：
GPU 租⾦持续飙升，背后可能不是 AI 算⼒需求有多强，⽽是融资渠道收紧造成的供给收缩。
Liquid Compute ⾸席产品官 Stanley Lee 近期在 Odd Lots 播客中指出，当前 GPU 现货和短期租⾦
更多反映资⾦成本，⽽⾮底层需求变化。贷款机构收紧对中型新型云服务商（ neocloud ）的融
资条件，迫使⽆法获得⻓期贷款的买家转向短期现货市场，反⽽推⾼了可⻅租⾦。
融资条件的变化已经相当明显。贷款机构对 neocloud 的贷款价值⽐（ LTV ）已从今年夏季的 80%
降⾄⽬前的 50% ⾄ 60% 。与此同时， CoreWeave 信⽤违约互换（ CDS ）利差扩⼤⾄约 1300 个基

点，其 2031 年到期债券收益率升⾄ 16% 。
⾼盛交易台主管 Rich Privorotsky 此前将 GPU 租⾦称为 AI 交易中最重要的单⼀指标，但⾼盛如今
也承认，现货租⾦虽然受到物理稀缺性⽀撑，底层经济性却在恶化。真正的问题是：租⾦上涨
究竟来⾃客⼾业务增⻓，还是来⾃上⼀轮股权融资带来的购买⼒？
信贷收紧推⾼ GPU 租⾦：融资越难，现货越贵
GPU 租⾦近期涨势显著。Silicon Data 的 B200 指数从 8 ⽉底⾄ 11 ⽉ 19 ⽇上涨 23.5% ，由每 GPU
⼩时 5.62 美元升⾄ 6.94 美元，周⼆⼀度触及 7.10 美元。 H100 租⾦⾃ 2025 年 7 ⽉以来上涨 67% 。
戴尔创始⼈ Michael Dell 上周也表⽰， H100 每⼩时租⾦已从⼀年前的 1.96 美元升⾄ 3.10 美元。
但租⾦上涨的背后，融资⻔槛正在快速提⾼。
Stanley Lee 表⽰，中型 neocloud 通常以 GPU 作抵押，获得数亿美元、期限三年的全额摊还贷

款，并依靠 AI 客⼾签订的照付不议（ take-or-pay ）合同还款。今年夏季，这类贷款的 LTV 最⾼可
达 80% ，劳动节后降⾄ 70% ，⽬前贷款机构普遍要求降⾄ 50% ⾄ 60% ， 50% LTV 贷款的利率报
价已达到⼗⼏个百分点的中⾼位。
以 1 亿美元的 GPU 集群为例， neocloud 需要⾃⾏筹集的资⾦已从 3000 万美元增加⾄最⾼ 5000 万
美元，增幅约 67% 。此前，不少⼩型运营商依靠客⼾预付款填补资⾦缺⼝，如今则不得不要求
客⼾追加预付款，或转向股权融资。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

融资收紧直接改变了市场供需：运营商难以获得⻓期贷款，新集群建设放缓；⽆法签订⻓期合
同的买家转向短期现货市场，推⾼租⾦。即使终端需求没有增⻓，这⼀过程也⾜以制造租⾦上
涨⾏情。
初创公司宁愿⽀付更⾼租⾦，也不愿提前掏钱
融资压⼒还改变了 AI 初创公司的采购⽅式。 Lee 举例称，同⼀客⼾⾯对两种 B300 算⼒租赁⽅
案：⼀种每 GPU ⼩时 4.40 美元，但需要⽀付 30% ⾸付款；另⼀种⽆需⾸付，但每⼩时租⾦约为
5.65 美元。客⼾往往更愿意选择后者。
按照三年、每天 24 ⼩时计算，前⼀种⽅案总费⽤约 11.56 万美元，⾸付款约 3.47 万美元；后⼀种
⽅案总费⽤约 14.85 万美元。为了省下前期资⾦，客⼾需要额外⽀付约 3.3 万美元，隐含年化融资
成本约为 30% ⾄ 55% 。
这种选择反映出初创公司的资⾦困境：现⾦储备往往⽐融资成本更重要。保留现⾦可以延⻓运
营时间，⽽增加算⼒则有助于扩⼤业务规模、争取收⼊增⻓，并为下⼀轮⻛险投资融资创造条
件。
但这也意味着， GPU 租⾦上涨未必代表客⼾有更强的盈利能⼒，部分需求实际上仍由外部股权

融资⽀撑。
租⾦上涨不等于需求强劲：市场价格与实际价值背离
GPU 租赁市场的报价并不统⼀。 Bernstein 在分析 CoreWeave 关于闲置 GPU 转租每年可创造 100
亿美元收⼊的说法时，列出了同⼀天四家供应商的 H100 报价：每⼩时 3.50 美元、 3.30 美元、
3.01 美元和 2.80 美元，最⾼与最低相差 25% ，⽽ CoreWeave 管理层采⽤的价格假设为 3.81 美
元。 Ornn 的 H100 指数也已从 11 ⽉初约 3.50 美元回落⾄ 2.92 美元。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

图⽚
Lee 警告，如果租⾦上涨是因为信贷变得更难获得，⽽不是终端需求增强，那么这种⾏情并不健
康。⼀旦融资渠道进⼀步收窄、运营商现⾦耗尽，市场价格可能迅速下跌。
私募信贷机构已经开始关注这⼀⻛险。过去两周， Liquid Compute 每天接到⼀⾄三通⼤型私募
信贷基⾦的电话，询问 B300 租赁指数的看跌期权，⽽六个⽉前⼏乎没有类似需求。与此同时，
⼤型 neocloud 也开始打听能否在⼩型竞争对⼿折价出售资产时接⼿ GPU 集群。
更值得警惕的是， GPU 租⾦上涨的同时，算⼒产出的价格却在下跌。⾃ 7 ⽉以来， Ornn 的
OpenAI token 价格指数已从 1.20 美元以上降⾄ 0.31 美元， Anthropic 指数从约 2 美元降⾄ 0.77 美

元。英伟达周⼆也表⽰， token 价格⾃ 5 ⽉以来下跌近 60% 。投⼊成本上涨、产出价格下跌，正
在挤压算⼒运营商的利润空间。
⻛投资⾦退潮后， GPU 租⾦可能迎来反转
当前 AI 算⼒需求的核⼼隐忧，是买家的⽀付能⼒仍然⾼度依赖⻛险投资。 Lee 指出，判断算⼒需
求是否可持续，关键在于客⼾能否从股权融资转向业务收⼊⽀付算⼒账单。除 Anthropic 等少数
公司外，⽬前尚缺乏⾜够公开数据证明这⼀转变已经发⽣。
Rothschild Redburn 分析师 Alexander Haissl 此前也指出， CoreWeave 和 Nebius 等公司的算⼒价
格虽然上涨，但出价最⾼的客⼾往往是⻛险投资⽀持的初创公司，其需求能否持续取决于外部
资本能否持续注⼊。英伟达⼀直是这⼀融资体系的重要资⾦来源，但公司临时 CEO Colette
Kress 周⼆表⽰，英伟达仍将⽀持 neocloud ，同时会采取更加审慎的选择标准。
融资规模同样不容忽视。⾼盛信⽤策略团队预计， 2027 年芯⽚与算⼒融资规模将达到 3200 亿美

元，接近今年⽔平的七倍。播客嘉宾 Paul Kedrosky 则警告，当前 AI 投资体系扩张迅速，但⼀旦
资⾦链逆转，收缩速度也可能远超预期。
危险在于，⼀旦⻛投资⾦放缓、客⼾停⽌付款，运营商可能违约，贷款机构收回的 GPU 集群⼜
将重新流⼊市场。届时，新增供给恰逢需求减弱，当前由信贷收紧推⾼的租⾦可能迅速反转。

## Document 296: Brent at $77_bbl_ 3 calls for energy markets

Source PDF: all report pdf/Brent at $77_bbl_ 3 calls for energy markets.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Given the level of uncertainty in energy markets this year and
with little sign of a change going into next year, any call on
energy markets for 2027 is a low-conviction call. But here are
our 3 calls for energy markets for 2027
Warren Patterson
Oil ﬂows are recovering, but uncertainty remains
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING's base call: Brent to average
$77/bbl next year

### Page 2

Oil markets have been on a roller coaster this year given
events in the Middle East. However, we are going into year-
end with oil ﬂows from the region near pre-war levels despite
the lack of a deal between the US and Iran. While oil ﬂows are
normalising, the way oil is coming out of the region is
anything but normal, which is reﬂected in the surge in tanker
shipping rates, given the very ineﬃcient oil ﬂows.
Our base case assumes occasional ﬂare-ups between the US

and Iran, meaning a full normalisation of Persian Gulf
supplies remains some way oﬀ and the oil market is likely to
retain a degree of risk premium. However, provided there are
no signiﬁcant or prolonged disruptions, we expect the market
surplus to build over the course of 2027, leaving Brent to
average around $77/bbl. A deal between the US and Iran
would add further downside risk to this view.
Our risky call: OPEC will see more member
exits
We believe harmony within OPEC could be tested, with the

possibility of further members leaving the group. This follows the
UAE’s departure earlier this year. Post-Persian Gulf disruptions, any
breakdown or weakening of the group risks adding more supply to
the market, which would potentially leave further downside to our
base case price forecasts for Brent in 2027.
The most likely candidate to leave OPEC is Venezuela, with the US’s
growing inﬂuence over the Venezuelan oil industry following the
capture and arrest of President Maduro at the start of 2026. But we

believe that some Persian Gulf producers may also question their
membership, particularly if disagreements emerge over the
capacity numbers used by the group to set production targets. Iraq
has already made noise around production targets, and following
the exit of the UAE, some members may be looking at them
enviously, with the UAE pumping record volumes of crude since
leaving the bloc.

Our bold call: TTF to trade sub-€40/MWh
before the end of next year
European gas prices have strengthened signiﬁcantly in 2026. But

what goes up must come down, and we believe prices could fall
sharply next year. There are clearly plenty of assumptions here,
most importantly a normalisation of Persian Gulf LNG ﬂows in
early 2027. If that happens, we believe TTF could trade below
€40/MWh by end-2027.
On the demand side, higher prices are already driving some
industrial demand destruction, while we are deep in the gas-to-coal
switching range. Furthermore, El Niño could bring milder weather to

parts of Europe and East Asia. While far from guaranteed, this could
reduce heating demand in winter. So, we potentially come out of
the 2026/27 winter with the EU gas balance not as tight as many
fear, putting downward pressure on prices.
We also have signiﬁcant US LNG export capacity coming onto the
market, which should help with storage ahead of the 2027/28
winter. If the situation in the Persian Gulf normalises, we also have
new capacity starting up in Qatar.

Share

## Document 326: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53.2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities in the coming 6-12 months, 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15-20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid-to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid-cycle bull market. Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex-UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low-teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities. We expect the 
segment to generate 10-15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years, with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High-Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks, MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advances in oncology and innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks 
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. Healthcare and 
consumer staples accounts for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management in a mid-
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and potentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge 
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two, and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets, private equity provides a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, infrastructure offers differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where contracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3-7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell-off caused by AI-
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi-sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010-2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

## Document 356: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher
costs and formalising data centres' responsibility to help fund grid
and energy infrastructure upgrades. Such measures could be added
to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like
permitting reform.
More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase
scrutiny of technology companies, as discussed here, while utilising

the next two years to prepare an AI governance framework for 2028
and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH FROM LSEG

This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 30, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 147 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 17 positive

surprises and 11 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

## Document 386: New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-23).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• As polls suggested, the election was a nailbiter
that has not yet been resolved. Election night
results show a lead to the centre-right on
paper in a significantly enlarged parliament.
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First
and ACT won 67 seats. The centre-left bloc
of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti Māori won 57
seats. There was a swing away from National
to its coalition partners while the centre left
grouping picked up a few seats.

• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling
and will enter parliament with at least 7 seats,
reducing what could have been a larger swing
to the centre-left grouping.
• The size of parliament will expand by around 9
seats as National held onto a large share of its
electorate votes, entitling it to more seats than
implied by its party vote share. As a result,
a bloc would require 66 seats to command a
majority in parliament.
• Several senior National ministers have not

been re-elected, including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. She will continue in a caretaker
role but will need to be replaced in any new
centre-right government.
• Significant uncertainty about the result exists.
National might lose perhaps two marginal
electorates in the final analysis. There are
400,000 special votes to count which may
shift the list seat allocations as these tend
to reduce the share of the centre-right bloc
by 1.6-1.7ppts. Hence, when all is said and

done, the centre-right’s 10 seat advantage on
election night could reduce by 1-4 seats.
• Should this play out the centre-right would not
have sufficient seats to reach a majority in an
enlarged house and would require the support
of the Opportunity Party to govern.
• The final election outcome will not be
known until 27 November. Serious coalition
negotiations likely won’t begin until then
and delays could occur. Should recounts be
required, some further delay is possible.

• Based on these results, we see a higher
probability of a centre-right government
ultimately emerging that likely will require
the support of the Opportunity Party in some
form. The Opportunity Party likely won’t enter
Cabinet but may offer support on confidence
and supply in exchange for policy concessions.
• But a centre-left government can’t be ruled
out if special votes heavily favour the centre
left bloc, and especially if an agreement
between the Opportunity Party and the centre-

right parties can’t be reached.
• Regardless of the final count, the results
suggest a government with weak support and
ongoing vulnerabilities. Hence the prospect of
controversial large changes may be modest.
For a centre-right government that might
limit the extent of fiscal consolidation, while
the centre left’s plans for larger scale tax and
spending initiatives could be trimmed back.
• A centre-right government won’t change the
RBNZ mandate while a centre-left government

likely still will. A new finance minister will be
required with the possibility of ACT leader
David Seymour taking on that role.

New Zealand 2026 General Election:
Preliminary results and next steps.
9 Nov 2026 |  Westpac Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz
Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
The result.
The preliminary results of the 2026 General Election,
based on the election night count, are set out in the table
above. In summary:
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First and ACT
won 67 seats.
• The centre-left bloc of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti
Māori won 57 seats.
• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling and will
enter parliament with 7 seats.
These results indicate a swing away from National to its

coalition partners while the centre-left grouping picked
up a few seats. The big gainer was the Opportunity Party
who perhaps took a big share of what otherwise could
have been a larger swing to the centre-left grouping.
The size of parliament will expand by around nine seats,
as National held onto a large share of its electorate
votes and hence has more seats than it would have been
entitled to based on its party vote share. As a result, a
governing bloc would require 66 seats to command a

majority in parliament.
Several of the electorate seats are very tight (see Table
2), so election night majorities could easily be overturned
once special votes are counted. Of particular relevance
is the seat of Wairarapa, where the National candidate is
leading the Labour candidate by just 139 votes. If Labour
were to win that seat (specials favoured Labour in 2020
but National in 2023), all else equal the centre-right seat
count would decline to 66 seats in what would become

a 130-seat Parliament – just enough to command a
majority. But if either NZ First or ACT were to lose a single
party vote seat to the left bloc once specials are counted
then that majority would disappear.
On that score, historically the election night results have
tended to overstate the support of the centre-right bloc
and understate the support for parties on the left of
the political spectrum. For example, over the previous
three elections (2017, 2020, 2023), the current centre-

right bloc’s vote share has declined 1.9%, 1.7% and 1.6%
respectively following the counting of special votes, with
the National Party losing 2 seats on each occasion once
the official results were declared (Labour, the Greens and
Te Pāti Māori have benefited). On this occasion, given its
overhang, a lower party vote share would not lower the
National Party’s final vote count, although it could alter
the size of its overhang and thus the number of seats

required to command a majority. In recent elections both
NZ First and ACT have seen their vote shares decline
following the counting of special votes.
The process from here.
Official results.
The official results will be declared on 27 November,
adding the results of an estimated 400,000 special votes
(with around a quarter of these cast overseas) to the
2.41m votes counted on election night.
We expect discussions between potential coalition
partners will begin immediately to start sketching out the

parameters of a coalition agreement. But given the tight
margins of the current vote count, no firm positions will
be agreed until the results are declared.
The writ and recounts.
After 27 November, the writ is scheduled to be returned
on 3 December – formally certifying who has been
elected. However, that date is subject to any recounts

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
that might be requested. Parliament is required to meet
no later than 14 January (six weeks after the return of the
writ), providing an opportunity for a new government
to test that it has the confidence of the House. It is
worth noting that Parliament can be summoned and
begin functioning even if coalition negotiations are still
occurring. If no coalition or confidence-and-supply
arrangement has been reached by then, negotiations can

simply continue and the incumbent Government remains
in office under the caretaker convention (Ministers in
the outgoing government retain executive authority
temporarily, even if they are no longer MPs).
Given that the Pre-Election Economic and Fiscal
Update was released in late September there is no legal
requirement for the Treasury to publish a Half Year
Economic and Fiscal Update (HYEFU) this year. And given
that it will be some weeks before a government is formed,

it seems unlikely that there will be a government in place
that would request that a HYEFU be produced despite
there being no statutory requirement (certainly there
would be little time to incorporate policy changes that
fall out of new coalition agreements, thus diminishing
its value). Once a government is formed, it will have an
opportunity to lay out its immediate priorities in the
Budget Policy Statement (BPS), which by law needs to
be released before the end of March next year. The new

government could decide to release an interim fiscal
update with the BPS if that was thought necessary.
Potential coalition outcomes.
We see three main likely outcomes:
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First.
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First with the support of the Opportunity Party
on confidence and supply.
• Some coalition of Labour, the Greens, Opportunity and
Te Pāti Māori, potentially with Opportunity and Te Pāti

Māori sitting outside the cabinet.
Our preview of the election discussed the policies of
the main political parties, while the figure on page 5
highlights some points of commonality with the centre-
right and centre-left blocs.
National, ACT and NZ First share a broadly pro-
development and deregulatory agenda, including support
for greater resource development, fewer regulatory
barriers and compulsory or expanded KiwiSaver. Beyond
that common core, National and ACT align particularly

closely on lower taxes and deregulation, while National
and NZ First share an emphasis on infrastructure, regional
development and law and order. ACT and NZ First favour
tighter immigration settings and tighter welfare policies.
The differences are clearest at the edges: ACT advocates
deeper tax cuts, smaller government and more market-
led provision, while NZ First places greater emphasis
on economic sovereignty, regional development and
targeted support for working New Zealanders. National

occupies more of the middle ground, combining fiscal
discipline and a pro-business agenda with increased
infrastructure and housing supply.
The Opportunity Party presents itself as a middle
alternative that can work with the left or the right
groupings. They have five bottom line policies they say
they need to support any new government. These include
competition reform through enhanced Commerce
Commission powers, more restrictions on political
lobbyists and donors, a ten-year plan of investment in the

healthcare sector, an end to bottom trawling in inshore
areas and a phase out elsewhere and for the Government
to recommit to New Zealand’s Paris Agreement
obligations. Their success in meeting these bottom
lines with a new government and their flexibility around
these requirements will be key to their ability to form an
agreement with either side. There is better congruence
of the Opportunity Party’s policies with the left than the
right – so progress here will be critical to watch should

their support be required to form a new government. We
suspect they will be very motivated to avoid the need for
a new election via a hung parliament should push come
to shove.

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Labour, the Greens and Opportunity share support
for increased public and social investment, a broader
tax base, greater infrastructure investment, stronger
competition policy, cheaper public transport and a
more active role for government. Labour and the Greens
overlap particularly on higher taxation, public investment
and stronger climate and environmental policy, while
Labour and Opportunity share an emphasis on social

investment and higher public-service wages. The Greens
and Opportunity both favour broader taxation and
stronger climate action, although their preferred tax
instruments differ. Their distinctive priorities are also
clear: Labour places more weight on worker protections
and restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate; the Greens on
environmental protection, progressive taxation and Te
Tiriti; and Opportunity on land-value taxation, housing
infrastructure, productivity, innovation and evidence-

based policy.

and differences.

## Document 412: TCT Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam - Báo cáo cập nhật 3Q2026 (2026-11-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/TCT Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam - Báo cáo cập nhật 3Q2026 (2026-11-19).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Company)
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

TCT Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam (ACV) 
Áp lực lợi nhuận trong chu kỳ mở rộng công suất 
19/11/2026

KQKD 3Q2026 suy giảm do khấu hao 
và biến động tỷ giá

Lượt khách ACV dự kiến tăng chậm 
hơn toàn ngành do tác động từ Phú 
Quốc và Gia Bình

Long Thành dự kiến khai thác từ cuối 
2026, tạo áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận 
giai đoạn đầu

Duy trì khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá 
mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu

3Q2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu đạt 6,066 tỷ VND (-6% yoy) và LNST công ty mẹ 
đạt 2,206 tỷ VND (-14% yoy). Lợi nhuận suy giảm chủ yếu do (1) khấu hao tăng từ 
các nhà ga mới và (2) ghi nhận lỗ tỷ giá 168 tỷ VND trong kỳ, trái ngược với khoản lãi 
tỷ giá cùng kỳ 2025. Lũy kế 9M2026, doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ lần lượt đạt 
19,226/7,435 tỷ VND (+0%/+2% yoy).

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế/nội địa qua hệ thống ACV tăng trưởng bình 
quân 7%/5.5%/năm đến 2030, thấp hơn toàn ngành do (1) sân bay Phú Quốc 
không còn thuộc hệ thống ACV từ đầu 2026 và (2) một phần khách quốc tế tại Nội 
Bài dịch chuyển tự nhiên sang sân bay Gia Bình khi đi vào khai thác. Mức độ dịch 
chuyển trước năm 2030 dự kiến còn hạn chế do phụ thuộc vào tiến độ hoàn thiện 
sân bay và hạ tầng giao thông kết nối.

Cảng HKQT Long Thành giai đoạn 1 dự kiến khai thác một phần từ cuối tháng 
12/2026 và bắt đầu tiếp nhận các chuyến bay quốc tế đường dài từ Tân Sơn Nhất 
vào ngày 28/03/2027, bổ sung công suất 25 triệu khách/năm cho ACV. Tuy nhiên, 
tốc độ chuyển dịch có thể chậm hơn kế hoạch do hạ tầng kết nối chưa hoàn thiện, 
trong khi chi phí khấu hao và vận hành tăng dự kiến gây áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận 
của ACV giai đoạn 2027–2028.

Sử dụng phương pháp chiết khấu dòng tiền, chúng tôi duy trì khuyến nghị TRUNG 
LẬP đối với ACV với giá mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng 
giá 4.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 18/11/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh

ACV hiện đang quản lý, đầu tư và khai thác 
hệ thống 21 Cảng hàng không trong cả nước, 
bao gồm 10 Cảng hàng không quốc tế và 11 
Cảng hàng không nội địa. Ngành nghề kinh 
doanh chính của ACV là hoạt động cung cấp 
dịch vụ hỗ trợ trực tiếp cho vận tải hàng 
không, bao gồm dịch vụ hàng không, dịch vụ 
phi hàng không và bán hàng. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Tăng trưởng bền vững đến từ cả nhóm khách nội địa và quốc tế, tốc độ tăng trung bình dự kiến đạt 
5.5% và 7%/năm giai đoạn 2026-2030. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Long Thành bổ sung 25 triệu khách/năm từ 2027, song khấu hao tăng mạnh khiến lợi nhuận chạm 
đáy năm 2028 trước khi phục hồi từ 2029. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Quá trình lên HOSE của ACV tiến triển thuận lợi nhờ các tài sản nhà nước đang dần được tách bạch, 
tạo thuận lợi cho việc hoàn tất cổ phần hóa tài sản khu bay cho phần vốn nhà nước. 
 Tham khảo trong báo cáo kỳ trước tại đây
